Supplementary Material

Contents

S1
Recap: Loop and Cluster expansion

S1.1
Preliminaries

Given a graph
G=(V,E)
, we associate to each edge
e\in E
a
bond space

\mathcal{B}_{e}
of dimension
\dim(\mathcal{B}_{e})=D
, where
D
is called the bond dimension, chosen to be uniform for convenience. For each vertex
v\in V
, We denote the neighbors of
v
in
G
as
\mathcal{N}(v)
. The degree of a vertex in the graph is denoted
d(v):=|\mathcal{N}(v)|
. The degree of a graph is denoted
\Delta(G):=\max_{v\in V}d(v)
. With this, we define the notion of a tensor network.

Definition S1.1
[Tensor Network]
.

A graph
G=(V,E)
equipped with a set of tensors at each vertex,
\mathcal{T}=\{T_{v}\}_{v}
with
T_{v}\in\otimes_{e\ni v}\mathcal{B}_{e}
is called a
tensor network
.

Now, the BP algorithm proceeds by associating two messages at each edge, i.e., for
e=(v,w)
we have
\mu_{v\to w},\mu_{w\to v}\in\mathcal{B}_{e}
. The messages are said to be self-consistent if they satisfy the following conditions.

Definition S1.2
[Self-consistent fixed point]
.

The self consistent set of messages
\mathcal{M}=\{\mu_{v\to w}\}_{v,w}
satisfies for each
v\in V
and each
n_{j}\in\mathcal{N}(v)
,

\left(\underset{n_{i}\in\mathcal{N}(v)/\{n_{j}\}}{\otimes}\mu_{n_{i}\to v}\right)\star T_{v}=\mu_{v\to n_{j}}

(S1)

For each edge
e=(v,w)\in E
, we expand the identity on the bond space
\mathbbm{1}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{B}_{e})
as:

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{vw}=\underbrace{\frac{\mu_{v\to w}\otimes\mu_{w\to v}}{I_{vw}}}_{\text{BP subspace}}+\underbrace{\mathcal{P}_{vw}^{\perp}}_{\text{excitation subspace}},

(S2)

where
I_{vw}=\mu_{v\to w}\star\mu_{w\to v}
. The normalization ensures that these subspaces are orthogonal:
\mathcal{P}_{vw}^{\perp}\star\mu_{v\to w}=0
and
\mu_{w\to v}\star\mathcal{P}_{vw}^{\perp}=0
, hence
\mathcal{P}_{vw}^{\perp}
carries contributions orthogonal to the BP vacuum.

Proposition S1.1
[BP Approximation]
.

For each
v\in V
, the local contribution to the partition function is then given as,

Z^{(v)}:=\left[\underset{n\in\mathcal{N}(v)}{\bigotimes}\frac{\mu_{n\to v}}{\sqrt{I_{vw}}}\right]\star T_{v},

(S3)

where
I_{vw}=\mu_{v\to w}\star\mu_{w\to v}
, that is the inner product between
\mu_{v\to w}
and
\mu_{w\to v}
. The BP approximation to

(i)

the partition function is,

Z_{\emph{BP}}=\prod_{v\in V}Z^{(v)}

(S4)

(ii)

the free energy is,

F_{\emph{BP}}=-\sum_{v\in V}\log{Z^{(v)} }

(S5)

We now recall the loop expansion
13
. The set of allowed loop excitations is denoted
\mathcal{L}
. For the naïve loop expansion,
\mathcal{L}
is the set of
connected
subgraphs with each vertex having degree at least two.

Definition S1.3
[Generalized loops]
.

Consider a graph
G=(V,E)
. A generalized loop is
connected
subgraph
l=(W,F)
with
W\subseteq V
,
F\subseteq E
, with the property that the degree of any
w\in W
in
C
is at least two. The weight of a generalized loop is defined as the number of edges
|F|
, also denoted
|l|
. The set of connected generalized loops is denoted
\mathcal{L}
.

Given a loop
l
, we define its support to be
\text{supp}(l)
the set of vertices in the associated subgraph. We can now define if two loops are compatible or incompatible.

Definition S1.4
[Compatible loops]
.

Two distinct loops
l,l^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}
are said to be
compatible
, written
l\sim l^{\prime}
, if they do not overlap; that is, they share no vertex or edge in the underlying graph. A family
\Gamma\subset\mathcal{L}
of loops is called
compatible
if every pair of distinct loops in
\Gamma
is compatible.

The loop expansion is expressed in a sum–product form over connected, compatible loop excitations in
\mathcal{L}
; as a consequence, it implicitly generates contributions from all disconnected loop configurations.

Proposition S1.2
[Loop expansion]
.

The tensor network contraction admits the expansion

\mathcal{Z}=Z_{\emph{BP}}\left(1+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Gamma\subseteq\mathcal{L}\\
\Gamma\emph{finite,compatible}\end{subarray}}\prod_{l\in\Gamma}Z_{l}\right)

(S6)

where the sum runs over all finite sets
\Gamma
of mutually compatible loops.

S1.2
Cluster expansion

We now define the notion of cluster. A cluster is a multiset of loops.

Definition S1.5
[Clusters]
.

Given the set of allowed excitations
\mathcal{L}
, a cluster is a collection of tuples of the form

\mathbf{W}=\{(l_{1},\eta_{1}),(l_{2},\eta_{2}),\ldots\}

where each
l_{i}\in\mathcal{L}
is a loop and
\eta_{i}
is the multiplicity of the loop
l_{i}
in the cluster. The total number of loops in the cluster is denoted as
n_{\mathbf{W}}=\sum_{i}\eta_{i}
.

We define the following properties of a cluster.

Definition S1.6
[Properties of a cluster]
.

For a cluster
\mathbf{W}=\{(l_{1},\eta_{1}),(l_{2},\eta_{2}),\ldots\}
, we define the following:

(i)

The
cluster weight
(or “
order
”) is defined as

|\mathbf{W}|=\sum_{i}\eta_{i}|l_{i}|,

(S7)

where the number of edges in excitation
l
be denoted as
|l|
.

(ii)

The
support
of a cluster is,

\emph{supp}(Z_{\mathbf{W}})=\cup_{l\in\mathbf{W}}\emph{supp}(l)

(S8)

(iii)

The
correction
of the cluster
Z_{\mathbf{W}}
as the product of the loop corrections raised to their respective multiplicities:

Z_{\mathbf{W}}=\prod_{i}Z_{l_{i}}^{\eta_{i}}.

(S9)

(iv)

The
loop-set
of a cluster is the set of loops which appear in the cluster with non-zero multiplicity. That is, for a cluster
\mathbf{W}=\{(l_{i},\eta_{i})\}_{i}
we have

\emph{loop-set}(\mathbf{W}):=\{l_{i}\,|\,\eta_{i}>0\}

(S10)

Given a cluster
\mathbf{W}
, we define the
interaction graph
as follows.

Definition S1.7
[Interaction Graph]
.

Given a cluster
\mathbf{W}=\{(l_{1},\eta_{1}),(l_{2},\eta_{2}),\ldots\}
, we define the interaction graph
G_{\mathbf{W}}=(V_{\mathbf{W}},E_{\mathbf{W}})
with
|V_{\mathbf{W}}|=\sum_{i}\eta_{i}
vertices with each loop
l_{i}
corresponds to
\eta_{i}
vertices. There is an edge
(l,l^{\prime})\in E_{\mathbf{W}}
either if the loops
l
and
l^{\prime}
are incompatible (
l\not\sim l^{\prime}
), or they are identical (
l=l^{\prime}
).

We call a cluster
\mathbf{W}

connected
if the interaction graph
G_{\mathbf{W}}
is connected, meaning there is a path between any two vertices in the interaction graph. The following two results are from Ref.
40
.

Proposition S1.3
[Connected clusters only]
.

The negative free energy can be expressed as

\log\mathcal{Z}=\log Z_{BP}+\sum_{\emph{connected}\,\mathbf{W}}\phi(\mathbf{W})Z_{\mathbf{W}},

(S11)

where the sum runs over all connected clusters
\mathbf{W}
. The coefficient
\phi(\mathbf{W})
is given by

\phi(\mathbf{W})=\frac{1}{\mathbf{W}!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}C\in G_{\mathbf{W}}\\
C\emph{connected}\end{subarray}}\sum_{(i,j)\in C}(-1)^{|E(C)|}

(S12)

Theorem S1.1
[Convergence of cluster expansion]
.

Assume there exists a constant
c>\log(2(\Delta-1))+\frac{1}{2}
such that

|Z_{l}|\leq e^{-c|l|}

(S13)

then, the series for
\log\mathcal{Z}
converges absolutely. Moreover, the error in truncating the series at order
m
is bounded by

\left|\mathcal{F}-{F}_{m}\right|\leq Ne^{-d(m+1)}

(S14)

where
d=c-\log(2(\Delta-1))-\frac{1}{2}
and
\Delta
is the degree of the graph.

The main technical argument in the proof of Theorem
S1.1
is the following tail-bound on the decay of large clusters.

Lemma S1.1
[Tail bound for large clusters]
.

Consider a PEPS satisfying the loop decay condition
|Z_{\ell}|\leq e^{-c|\ell|}
for
c>c_{0}=\log(2(\Delta-1))+1/2
. For any region
A\subset V
and cutoff
m\in\mathbb{N}
, the contribution from all connected clusters with weight higher than
m
supported on
A
satisfies:

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\emph{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\\
\emph{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
|\mathbf{W}|>m\end{subarray}}|\phi(\mathbf{W})Z_{\mathbf{W}}|\leq\order{|A|e^{-(c-c_0)(m+1)}}.

(S15)

S2
Cluster-cumulant expansion

We briefly introduce the setting of the Möbius transform
54
, used to convert the cluster expansion into the cluster-cumulant expansion in the main text.

S2.1
Preliminaries: Möbius transforms

Let
(P,\leq)
be a poset. The associated
incidence algebra
is

\mathcal{A}(P)=\bigl\{\alpha\in\mathbb{C}^{P\times P}:\alpha(x,y)=0\text{ unless }x\leq y\bigr\}.

For
\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal{A}(P)
, the product is defined by

(\alpha\beta)(x,y)=\sum_{z\in P}\alpha(x,z)\,\beta(z,y).

It is immediate that
\mathcal{A}(P)
is closed under addition, scalar multiplication, and multiplication.
Let
I\in\mathcal{A}(P)
denote the identity element,

I(x,y)=\begin{cases}1,&x=y,\\
0,&x\neq y,\end{cases}

so that
I
acts as the multiplicative identity in
\mathcal{A}(P)
.

The
zeta function
of the poset is

\zeta(x,y)=\begin{cases}1,&x\leq y,\\
0,&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

and clearly
\zeta\in\mathcal{A}(P)
.

The
Möbius function

\mu\in\mathcal{A}(P)
is defined recursively by

\mu(x,y)=\begin{cases}0,&x\not\leq y,\\
1,&x=y,\\
-\displaystyle\sum_{x\leq z<y}\mu(x,z),&x<y.\end{cases}

It satisfies

\sum_{x\leq z\leq y}\mu(x,z)=\begin{cases}1,&x=y,\\
0,&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

(S16)

which expresses the left-inverse relation
\mu*\zeta=I
.
Similarly, the right-inverse identity

\sum_{x\leq z\leq y}\mu(z,y)=\begin{cases}1,&x=y,\\
0,&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

(S17)

implies
\zeta*\mu=I
.
Thus
\mu
is the inverse of
\zeta
in
\mathcal{A}(P)
and is integer-valued by construction.

Theorem S2.1
[Möbius Inversion]
.

Let
(P,\leq)
be a poset with Möbius function
\mu
.
If two functions
f,g:P\to\mathbb{C}
are related by

g(x)=\sum_{y\leq x}f(y),

then

f(x)=\sum_{y\leq x}\mu(y,x)\,g(y).

(S18)

Proof.

Substituting the definition of
g
and using
Eq. (S16)
,

\displaystyle\sum_{y\leq x}\mu(y,x)g(y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\leq x}\mu(y,x)\Biggl(\sum_{z\leq y}f(z)\Biggr)=\sum_{z\leq y\leq x}\mu(y,x)f(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\leq x}f(z)\Biggl(\sum_{z\leq y\leq x}\mu(y,x)\Biggr)=f(x).

∎

Proposition S2.1
[Möbius Function on the Subset Lattice]
.

For the poset
P=\mathcal{P}([n])
ordered by inclusion, the Möbius function is given by

\mu(A,B)=\begin{cases}(-1)^{|B|-|A|},&A\subseteq B,\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proof.

By definition,
\mu(A,B)=0
if
A\not\subseteq B
. Now assume
A\subseteq B
. The interval
[A,B]=\{C:A\subseteq C\subseteq B\}
is isomorphic to the power set
\mathcal{P}(B\setminus A)
via the map
C\mapsto C\setminus A
. Since the Möbius function is an invariant of poset isomorphism, we have
\mu(A,B)=\mu(\emptyset,B\setminus A)
.

Let
s=|B\setminus A|
. We prove
\mu(\emptyset,S)=(-1)^{s}
by induction on
s
. For the base case: if
s=0
, then
S=\emptyset
and
\mu(\emptyset,\emptyset)=1=(-1)^{0}
.

Suppose the claim holds for all sets of size
k<s
. By the defining property of the Möbius function:

\sum_{\emptyset\subseteq C\subseteq S}\mu(\emptyset,C)=0\implies\mu(\emptyset,S)=-\sum_{\emptyset\subseteq C\subset S}\mu(\emptyset,C)

Applying the inductive hypothesis
\mu(\emptyset,C)=(-1)^{|C|}
and counting subsets by their size
i
:

\mu(\emptyset,S)=-\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}\binom{s}{i}(-1)^{i}

By the Binomial Theorem,
0=(1-1)^{s}=\sum_{i=0}^{s}\binom{s}{i}(-1)^{i}
. Thus, the sum from
0
to
s-1
must equal
-\binom{s}{s}(-1)^{s}=-(-1)^{s}
. Substituting this back:

\mu(\emptyset,S)=-(-(-1)^{s})=(-1)^{s}

It follows that
\mu(A,B)=(-1)^{|B\setminus A|}=(-1)^{|B|-|A|}
.
∎

For us,
|A|
will denote the number of generalized loops supported on region
A
.

S2.2
Cluster-cumulant expansion

Starting from the “standard” cluster expansion from
Proposition S1.3
,

\log\mathcal{Z}\;=\;\log Z_{BP}+\sum_{\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}}\phi(\mathbf{W})\,Z_{\mathbf{W}},

(S19)

each connected cluster
\mathbf{W}=\{(l_{i},\eta_{i})\}
has weight
Z_{\mathbf{W}}=\prod_{i}Z_{l_{i}}^{\eta_{i}}
and some loop-set
\text{loop-set}(\mathbf{W})\subseteq\mathcal{L}
.

Given a subset
\Gamma\subseteq\mathcal{L}
, we define the interaction graph
G_{\Gamma}
as that of the cluster
\mathbf{W}=\{(l,1)\}_{l\in\Gamma}
. We thus inherit the notion of connectedness of a set
\Gamma\subseteq\mathcal{L}
as before. Define, for every finite subset
\Gamma\subset\mathcal{L}
,

\mathfrak{K}(\Gamma)\;:=\;\begin{cases}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{W}\ \mathrm{connected}\\
\text{loop-set}(\mathbf{W})=\Gamma\end{subarray}}\phi(\mathbf{W})\,Z_{\mathbf{W}},&\text{if }G_{\Gamma}\text{ is connected},\\[12.91663pt]
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

(S20)

That is,
\mathfrak{K}(\Gamma)
collects all connected cluster contributions supported on
\Gamma
and vanishes whenever the induced subgraph
G_{\Gamma}
is disconnected.

Regrouping the sum in
Eq. (S19)
by the distinct supports
\Gamma
gives

\sum_{\text{connected }\mathbf{W}}=\sum_{\Gamma\subset\mathcal{L}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{W}\ \text{connected}\\
\text{loop-set}(\mathbf{W})=\Gamma\end{subarray}},\qquad\log\mathcal{Z}-\log Z_{BP}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Gamma\subset\mathcal{L}\\
G_{\Gamma}\ \mathrm{connected}\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{K}(\Gamma),

so that the free energy can be written as a sum purely over finite sets.

For any finite
B\subset\mathcal{L}
, define the
restricted partition function

\Xi(B)=1+\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Gamma^{\prime}\subseteq B\\
\Gamma^{\prime}\ \mathrm{compatible}\end{subarray}}\!\prod_{l\in\Gamma^{\prime}}Z_{l}.

(S21)

The cluster expansion implies

\log\Xi(B)=\sum_{\Gamma\subseteq B}\mathfrak{K}(\Gamma),

(S22)

which has the structure of a cumulative function over the subset lattice of
B
.
Applying the Möbius inversion
Theorem S2.1
together with
Proposition S2.1
yields the
cumulant-cluster formula

\mathfrak{K}(A)=\sum_{B\subseteq A}(-1)^{|A|-|B|}\,\log\Xi(B).

(S23)

Theorem S2.2
[Cluster-cumulant expansion]
.

The free energy can be written as the sum of cumulants of all connected subgraphs
\Gamma
of
\mathcal{L}
.

\boxed{\;\log\mathcal{Z}=\log Z_{BP}+\sum_{\Gamma\subseteq\mathcal{L}}\mathfrak{K}(\Gamma).\;}

(S24)

where the cumulants can be written down as the inclusion-exclusion sum of local partition functions, viz.,

\displaystyle\Xi(B)

\displaystyle=1+\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Gamma^{\prime}\subseteq B\\
\Gamma^{\prime}\ \mathrm{compatible}\end{subarray}}\!\prod_{l\in\Gamma^{\prime}}Z_{l}

(S25)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}(\Gamma)

\displaystyle=\sum_{B\subseteq\Gamma}(-1)^{|\Gamma|-|B|}\,\log\Xi(B).

(S26)

The support of a set of loops
\Gamma
, is defined as the union of support of the individual loops,

\text{supp}(\Gamma):=\cup_{l\in\Gamma}\text{supp}(l)

(S27)

S2.3
Examples

We illustrate the computation of the cumulants for a few simple cases.

•

For
A=\{l\}
,

\Xi(A)=1+Z_{l},\qquad\mathfrak{K}(A)=\log(1 + Z_l).

•

For
A=\{l_{1},l_{2}\}
with
l_{1}\not\sim l_{2}
(incompatible),

\Xi(A)=1+Z_{l_{1}}+Z_{l_{2}},\qquad\mathfrak{K}(A)=\log(1 + Z_{l_1} + Z_{l_2})-\log(1 + Z_{l_1})-\log(1 + Z_{l_2}).

•

For
A=\{l_{1},l_{2}\}
with
l_{1}\sim l_{2}
(compatible),

\Xi(A)=(1+Z_{l_{1}})(1+Z_{l_{2}}),\qquad\mathfrak{K}(A)=0.

S2.4
Radius of convergence

We can now attempt truncating the cluster-cumulant series at a finite weight
|\Gamma|\leq m
, where for
\Gamma=\{l_{1},l_{2},\dots\}
we have
|\Gamma|=|l_{1}|+|l_{2}|+\dots
.

F_{m}=-\log Z_{BP}-\sum_{\text{conn.}|\Gamma|\leq m}\mathfrak{K}(\Gamma).

(S28)

Lemma S2.1.

The cluster-cumulant expansion has the same radius of convergence as the cluster expansion.

Proof.

We have that,

|F_{m}-\mathcal{F}|=\sum_{\text{conn.}|\Gamma|\geq m+1}\mathfrak{K}(\Gamma).

(S29)

This can be upper bounded straightforwardly from the bound on
Z_{\mathbf{W}}
. Particularly, fix a site
v
, and we have for all connected sets
\Gamma
supported on that site,

\displaystyle\sum_{\Gamma\not\sim v:|\Gamma|\geq m+1}|\mathfrak{K}(\Gamma)|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\Gamma:|\Gamma|\geq m+1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{W}\ \mathrm{connected}\\
\text{loop-set}(\mathbf{W})=\Gamma\\
\mathbf{W}\not\sim i\end{subarray}}|\phi(\mathbf{W})\,Z_{\mathbf{W}}|

(S30)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=m+1}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected }\,\mathbf{W}\\
\mathbf{W}\not\sim i\\
|\mathbf{W}|=k\end{subarray}}|\phi(\mathbf{W})Z_{\mathbf{W}}|\leq\frac{1}{2}e^{-d(m+1)}

(S31)

where
d=(c-c_{0})>0
by decay of loops. From a union bound over all the sites in the lattice, we get,

|F_{m}-\mathcal{F}|\leq Ne^{-d(m+1)}

(S32)

∎

S2.5
“Top-down” expansion

As noted in the main text, there are two different ways to view the cluster-cumulant expansion. One is through a Möbius transform as above. This construction is “bottom up”: the cumulant
\mathfrak{K}(\Gamma)
is defined solely through the subsets of
\Gamma
, per
Eq. (S26)
.

Alternatively, we can reorganize the truncated series
Eq. (S28)
as in
subsection II.5
, repeated below for convenience
73
:

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathrm{conn.}|\Gamma|\leq m}\mathfrak{K}(\Gamma)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathrm{conn.}|\Gamma|\leq m}\sum_{B\subseteq\Gamma}(-1)^{|\Gamma|-|B|}\log\Xi(B)

(S33a)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\mathrm{conn.}|B|\leq m}b_{m}(B)\log\Xi(B)

(S33b)

where

b_{m}(B):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathrm{conn}.\Gamma\supseteq B\\
|\Gamma|\leq m\end{subarray}}\mu(B,\Gamma)=1-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathrm{conn.}\Gamma\supset B\\
|\Gamma|\leq m\end{subarray}}b_{m}(\Gamma)

(S34)

The astute reader may question why we need only consider connected subsets
B
in
Eq. (S33b)
, whereas the sum defining the cumulant (
Eq. (S26)
) includes all subsets of
\Gamma
, including disconnected ones. The reason is simply that the restricted partition function (
Eq. (S21)
) for a disconnected subgraph
\Gamma
factorizes, so we can account for the contribution of a disconnected subgraph simply by adjusting the counting numbers of its connected components. To be concrete: suppose we included a disconnected subset
B=B_{1}\cup B_{2}
in the sum
Eq. (S33b)
, with counting number
\tilde{b}_{m}(B)
. This modifies

b_{m}(B_{1})\rightarrow\tilde{b}_{m}(B_{1})=b_{m}(B_{1})-b_{m}(B),\quad b_{m}(B_{2})\rightarrow\tilde{b}_{m}(B_{2})=b_{m}(B_{2})-b_{m}(B),

while leaving all other counting numbers unchanged. This redefinition of the counting numbers leaves the partition function invariant, as

\tilde{b}_{m}(B)\log\Xi(B)+\tilde{b}_{m}(B_{1})\log\Xi(B_{1})+\tilde{b}_{m}(B_{2})\log\Xi(B_{2})=b_{m}(B_{1})\log\Xi(B_{1})+b_{m}(B_{2})\log\Xi(B_{2}).

We therefore arrive at a repackaging of the order-
m
cluster-cumulant expansion for the free energy,

\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{0}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathrm{conn}.B\subseteq\mathcal{L}\\
|B|\leq m\end{subarray}}b_{m}(B)\log\Xi(B).

(S35)

Rather than organizing the expansion in terms of generalized loops, we can alternatively organize it in terms of
regions
: vertex-induced subgraphs of
G
containing up to
k
vertices. The restricted partition function on a region
R
is simply the contraction of the tensor network on that region, with BP messages inserted along its boundary. After normalizing the tensor network by the BP partition function, we exploit two facts: (1) the restricted partition function of an acyclic region vanishes, (2) the cumulant of a disconnected region vanishes
73
. Together, these properties allow us to write the expansion, analogously to
Eq. (S33b)
, as the weighted sum over restricted partition functions on connected, leafless, vertex-induced subgraphs of
G
,

F_{k}=-\log Z_{BP}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathrm{conn.}R\subseteq G\\
w(R)\leq k\end{subarray}}b_{k}(R)\log\Xi(R)\quad\mathrm{where}\quad b_{k}(R)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathrm{conn.}A\supset R\\
w(A)\leq k\end{subarray}}b_{k}(A),

(S36)

where
w(R)
denotes the number of vertices in
R
.
The recursive, top-down expression for the counting number – a manifestation of the inclusion-exclusion principle – also suggests an efficient way to identify the regions with nonzero counting numbers: first compute all maximal regions up to
k
vertices (regions that are not the subgraphs of any other
w\leq k
regions), then iteratively form intersections until no new regions are found. This procedure is outlined in
Alg. 1
. After the regions are obtained, all maximal regions are assigned
b_{k}(R)=1
, and the remaining counting numbers are determined recursively, starting from the largest regions.

Input :
Graph
G
, integer
k

Output :
Partially ordered set of regions
\mathcal{R}_{1},\mathcal{R}_{2},..
such that no region in
\mathcal{R}_{i}
is contained within a region in
\mathcal{R}_{j>i}

\mathcal{M}\leftarrow
connected, leafless, vertex-induced subgraphs of
G
containing up to
k
vertices;

1

\mathcal{R}_{1}\leftarrow

\{R\in\mathcal{M}\,|\,R\not\subset R^{\prime}\,\forall\,R^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}\}
(maximal/parent regions);

2

i\leftarrow
1;

3

while

\mathcal{R}_{i}
is not empty

do

4

\mathcal{R}_{i+1}\leftarrow\emptyset
;

5

for

R\in\mathcal{R}_{i}
,
R^{\prime}\in\mathcal{R}_{j\leq i}

do

6

P

\leftarrow
intersection of
R
and
R^{\prime}
;

7

if

P\notin\mathcal{R}_{i}

and

P
is connected and leafless
and

P
has at least one edge

then

8

Add
P
to
\mathcal{R}_{i+1}
;

9

end if

10

end for

11

i\leftarrow i+1
;

12

end while

13

14

return

\{\mathcal{R}_{1},...,\mathcal{R}_{i}\}
;

15

Algorithm 1

Region finding

S3
Local Observables

In this section, we state
Proposition III.1
and
Proposition III.2
in more detail and prove their convergence given loop decay (
Theorem II.1
).

The key idea we will use is the following: the cluster expansion works on top of a set of geometrically allowed excitations
\mathcal{L}
, with each excitation
l\in\mathcal{L}
associated with a weight
Z_{l}\in\mathbb{C}
. There is a hard-core pairwise interaction between the excitations, for
l,l^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}

\Delta(l,l^{\prime})=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $l$ and $l^{\prime}$ are incompatible},\\[4.0pt]
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

(S37)

The essential ingredient in what follows is a modification of the set of allowed excitations on the different TNs we consider.

In both expansions, we
consider a state
\ket{\psi}
described as a PEPS on a graph
G=(V,E)
. Consider the tensor network for the norm
\innerproduct{\psi}{\psi}
, denoted
\mathcal{Z}
. We find a set of fixed point messages
\mathcal{M}
for the norm network, and hence can expand the contraction,

\innerproduct{\psi}{\psi}=Z_{BP}\left(1+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Gamma\subset\mathcal{L}\\
\Gamma\text{ finite, compatible}\end{subarray}}\prod_{l\in\Gamma}Z_{l}\right)

(S38)

Now, consider an observable
O_{A}
with
\text{supp}(O_{A})\subseteq A
for
A\subset V
with
|A|=O(1)
. We can evaluate its expectation values in two different ways: (1) as the ratio of two partition functions or (2) as the derivative of a perturbed free energy. We consider these in turn.

S3.1
Ratio of partition functions

Let the TN corresponding to
\langle\psi|O_{A}|\psi\rangle
be denoted
\mathcal{Z}^{A}
. Consider expanding
\mathcal{Z}^{A}
with the fixed points
\mathcal{M}
of the norm network. Crucially, self-consistency is preserved except for all
v\in A
. It is easy to see if we define
\mathcal{L}_{A}
to be the set of subgraphs of
V
where each vertex in
V/A
has degree at least two, then we get,

\langle\psi|O_{A}|\psi\rangle=Z^{A}_{BP}\left(1+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Gamma\subset\mathcal{L}_{A}\\
\Gamma\text{ finite, compatible}\end{subarray}}\prod_{l\in\Gamma}Z_{l}\right)

(S39)

Note the following points: (i) for all
l\in\mathcal{L}_{A}
with
\text{supp}(l)\cap A=\emptyset
, we have
Z_{l}
identical to the corresponding loop contribution of Eq.
S38
, and (ii)
Z_{BP}^{A}
differs from
Z_{BP}
exactly inside
A
.

Proposition S3.1.

Let
\psi
be a state represented as a PEPS on
G=(V,E)
. Denote
\mathcal{Z}
to be the norm network
\langle\psi|\psi\rangle
. For
A\subset V
, let
\mathcal{Z}^{A}
denote the TN for
\langle\psi|O_{A}|\psi\rangle
with
\emph{supp}(O_{A})\subseteq A
. Given a cluster
\mathbf{W}
, denote the cluster correction
Z^{(A)}_{\mathbf{W}}
on
\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{Z}^{A})
respectively. Then we have that,

\expectationvalue{O_A}=\expectationvalue{O_A}_{\emph{BP}}\cdot\exp{\left(\sum_{\substack{\emph{connected} \, \mathbf{W} \leftarrow\LL_A \\
\emph{supp}(\mathbf{W}) \cap A \neq\emptyset}} \phi_{\mathbf{W}} (Z^A_{\mathbf{W}} - Z_{\mathbf{W}})\right)}

(S40)

Proof.

We have,

\expectationvalue{O_A}:=\frac{\langle\psi|O_{A}|\psi\rangle}{\innerproduct{\psi}{\psi}}

(S41)

Consider now
\log\expectationvalue{O_A}
. We can apply the cluster theorem to both the TNs separately with appropriate modification of the excitation set (
\mathcal{L}\to\mathcal{L}_{A}
), to get,

\log\expectationvalue{O_A}=\log\frac{Z^{A}_{BP}}{Z_{BP}}+\sum_{\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}Z^{A}_{\mathbf{W}}-\sum_{\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}Z_{\mathbf{W}}

(S42)

where we denote
Z
for loops/clusters on
\mathcal{Z}
and
Z^{A}
for loops/clusters on
\mathcal{Z}^{A}
. Note that,

\expectationvalue{O_A}_{\text{BP}}=\frac{Z^{A}_{BP}}{Z_{BP}}

(S43)

which depends on the messages on the boundary of
A
and the tensors in
A
. Now, all clusters not supported on
A
have identical
Z_{\mathbf{W}}
in both the summations, since we expanded with the same message tensors. Finally, we can replace
\mathcal{L}\to\mathcal{L}_{A}
for the denominator tensor for convenience, since all
l\in\mathcal{L}_{A}/\mathcal{L}
evaluate to zero anyway. Hence we get,

\log\expectationvalue{O_A}=\log\expectationvalue{O_A}_{\text{BP}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}(Z^{A}_{\mathbf{W}}-Z_{\mathbf{W}})

(S44)

Taking the exponential,

\expectationvalue{O_A}=\expectationvalue{O_A}_{\text{BP}}\cdot\exp{\left(\sum_{\substack{\text{connected} \, \mathbf{W} \leftarrow\LL_A \\
\supp(\mathbf{W}) \cap A \neq\emptyset}} \phi_{\mathbf{W}} (Z^A_{\mathbf{W}} - Z_{\mathbf{W}})\right)}

(S45)

∎

Re-organizing the cluster expansion with the cumulant method as in
Theorem S2.2
, we get the following cluster-cumulant expansion for local observables.

Corollary S3.1.

The cluster-cumulant version of the local operator expansion is given as,

\expectationvalue{O_A}=\expectationvalue{O_A}_{\emph{BP}}\cdot\exp{\left(\sum_{\substack{\emph{conn.} \Gamma\subseteq\LL_A \\
\emph{supp}(\Gamma) \cap A \neq\emptyset}} (\mathfrak K^A(\Gamma) - \mathfrak K(\Gamma))\right)}

(S46)

where the sum is over all connected subsets
\Gamma
of
\mathcal{L}_{A}
that intersect
A
.

Truncating the cluster expansion for a local observable at order
m
produces the estimate,

\expectationvalue{O_A}_{m}=\expectationvalue{O_A}_{\text{BP}}\cdot\exp{\sum_{\substack{\text{conn} \, \mathbf{W} \leftarrow\LL_A \\
\supp(\mathbf{W}) \cap A \neq\emptyset\\ |\mathbf W| \leq m}} \phi_{\mathbf{W}} (Z^A_{\mathbf{W}} - Z_{\mathbf{W}})}

(S47)

We show now that given decay of excitations on
\mathcal{L}_{A}
, this approximates the exact value up to relative error exponentially small with cluster size.

Theorem S3.1.

Given a local region
A\subset V
, assume decay of loops in
\mathcal{L}_{A}
on both
\mathcal{Z}=\langle\psi|\psi\rangle
and
\mathcal{Z}^{A}=\langle\psi|O_{A}|\psi\rangle
. Then, truncating the cluster expansion for local expectation values (in the setting of
Proposition S3.1
) leads to a relative error
\delta_{m}=|\expectationvalue{O_A}-\expectationvalue{O_A}_{m}|/|\expectationvalue{O_A}|
bounded by

\delta_{m}\leq\order{|A| e^{-(c-c_0)(m+1)} }

(S48)

where
d=c-c_{0}=\order{1}
.

Proof.

The relative error is by
Proposition S3.1
,

\delta_{m}:=\left|\frac{\expectationvalue{O_A}_{m}-\expectationvalue{O_A}}{\expectationvalue{O_A}}\right|=\left|\exp{\sum_{\substack{\text{conn} \, \mathbf{W} \leftarrow\LL_A \\
\supp(\mathbf{W}) \cap A \neq\emptyset\\ |\mathbf W| > m}} \phi_{\mathbf{W}} (Z^A_{\mathbf{W}} - Z_{\mathbf{W}})}-1\right|

(S49)

Note that for any
x\in\mathbb{C}
,

\displaystyle|e^{x}-1|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{x^{n}}{n!}\Big|

(S50)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{|x|^{n}}{n!}

(S51)

\displaystyle\leq e^{|x|}-1.

(S52)

Now, we bound the relative error as follows,

\displaystyle\delta_{m}

\displaystyle\leq\exp{\sum_{\substack{\text{conn} \, \mathbf{W} \leftarrow\LL_A \\
\supp(\mathbf{W}) \cap A \neq\emptyset\\ |\mathbf W| > m}} |\phi_{\mathbf{W}} (Z^A_{\mathbf{W}} - Z_{\mathbf{W}})|}-1

(S53)

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{X\in\{\emptyset,A\}}\exp{\sum_{\substack{\text{conn} \, \mathbf{W} \leftarrow\LL_A \\
\supp(\mathbf{W}) \cap A \neq\emptyset\\ |\mathbf W| > m}} |\phi_{\mathbf{W}} Z^X_{\mathbf{W}}|}-1

(S54)

\displaystyle\leq\exp{r|A| e^{-(c-c_0)(m+1)}}-1

(S55)

\displaystyle\leq r|A|e^{-(c-c_{0})(m+1)}\exp{r|A| e^{-(c-c_0)(m+1)}}

(S56)

where
r=\order{1}
is a constant and we used,

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{conn}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\ni A\\
|\mathbf{W}|>m\end{subarray}}|\phi_{\mathbf{W}}Z^{X}_{\mathbf{W}}|\leq\order{|A|e^{-(c-c_0)(m+1)}}

(S57)

by
Lemma S1.1
and the fact that
e^{x}-1\leq xe^{x}
.

For large
m
and
|A|=\order{1}
, the exponential prefactor is uniformly bounded by a constant
C=\order{1}
, and we get,

\delta_{m}\leq\order{|A| e^{-(c-c_0)(m+1)} }

(S58)

∎

S3.2
Derivative of free energy

In the derivative method, we instead consider the TN corresponding to
\langle\psi|\exp[\lambda O_{A}]|\psi\rangle
,
and let
\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}^{A}
denote its contraction. We have the following additive version of the local observable expansion.

Proposition S3.2.

Let
\psi
be a state represented as a PEPS on
G=(V,E)
. For
A\subset V
, let
\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}^{A}
denote the TN for
\langle\psi|\lambda O_{A}|\psi\rangle
with
\emph{supp}(O_{A})\subseteq A
, while
\mathcal{Z}^{A}
,
\mathcal{Z}
denote the TNs for
\langle\psi|O_{A}|\psi\rangle
and
\langle\psi|\psi\rangle
, respectively. Given a cluster
\mathbf{W}=\{(l_{1},\eta_{1}),(l_{2},\eta_{2}),...\}
, denote the cluster correction on
\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}^{A}
,
\mathcal{Z}^{A}
, and
\mathcal{Z}
, by
Z_{{\mathbf{W}},\lambda}^{A},Z_{\mathbf{W}}^{A},Z_{\mathbf{W}}
, respectively. Also denote
\mathbf{W}_{A}
the subset of loops within
\mathbf{W}
which intersect
A
. Then

\expectationvalue{O}_{A}=\partial_{\lambda}\log\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}^{A}\mid_{\lambda=0}\,=\expectationvalue{O_A}_{BP}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathrm{conn}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}\\
\mathrm{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}Z_{\mathbf{W}}\sum_{l\in\mathbf{W}_{A}}\eta_{l}\left[\frac{Z_{l}^{A}}{Z_{l}}-\expectationvalue{O_A}_{BP}\right].

(S59)

Proof.

The first equality follows immediately from the definition of
\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}^{A}
:

\partial_{\lambda}\log\langle\psi|\exp{\lambda O_A}|\psi\rangle\mid_{\lambda=0}\,=\frac{\langle\psi|O_{A}|\psi\rangle}{\bra{\psi}\psi\rangle}=\expectationvalue{O_A}.

(S60)

Next, we write the cluster expansion for
\log\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}^{A}
, using the message background

\mathcal{M}
which is self-consistent for
\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda=0}
, and the appropriately modified excitation set (
\mathcal{L}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}
):

\log\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}^{A}=\log Z_{BP,\lambda}^{A}+\sum_{\mathrm{conn.}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}Z_{{\mathbf{W}},\lambda}^{A}.

(S61)

Here,
Z_{{\mathbf{W}},\lambda}^{A}
is the partition function of the cluster (with anti-projectors defined on each edge according to the
\lambda=0
BP background), but with each tensor locally rescaled according to the perturbed BP partition function
Z_{BP,\lambda}^{A}
.

Without loss of generality, we can take
A
to be a single site; if
A
contains more than one vertex, we can merge them into a single “supervertex.” Define

\hbox to196.89pt{\vbox to50.82pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 57.66724pt\lower-25.41078pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{113.81146pt}{21.6241pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 157.48 -25.51 L 157.48 -15.75}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 157.48 -29.92 M 161.89 -29.92 C 161.89 -27.49 159.92 -25.51 157.48 -25.51 C 155.05 -25.51 153.07 -27.49 153.07 -29.92 C 153.07 -32.36 155.05 -34.33 157.48 -34.33 C 159.92 -34.33 161.89 -32.36 161.89 -29.92 Z M 157.48 -29.92}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{113.81146pt}{-21.6241pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
;
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

the local BP factor at vertex
v
with and without the insertion of an operator
O
, respectively. For notational convenience, let

z_{v,\lambda}=\begin{cases}z_{v}+\lambda z_{v}(O)&v=A\\
z_{v}&\mathrm{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(S62)

Then, the perturbed BP partition function is

Z_{BP,\lambda}^{A}=\prod_{v\in V}z_{v,\lambda}=(z_{A}+\lambda z_{A}(O))\prod_{v\in V/A}z_{v},

(S63)

and the associated cluster expansion is

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}^{A}

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in V}\log z_{v,\lambda}+\sum_{\text{conn}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}}\frac{\phi_{\mathbf{W}}\tilde{Z}_{{\mathbf{W}},\lambda}^{A}}{\prod_{l\in\mathbf{W}}\prod_{v\in l}z_{v,\lambda}^{\eta_{l}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in V}\log z_{v,\lambda}+\sum_{\text{conn}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}}\frac{\phi_{\mathbf{W}}{\prod_{l\in\mathbf{W}}{\left(\tilde{Z}_{{l},\lambda}^{A}\right)^{\eta_{l}}}}}{\prod_{l\in\mathbf{W}}\prod_{v\in l}z_{v,\lambda}^{\eta_{l}}}

(S64)

where
\tilde{Z}_{{\mathbf{W}},\lambda}^{A}
denotes the unnormalized cluster weight, and
\tilde{Z}_{{l},\lambda}^{A}
the unnormalized loop correction for loop
l
.

We are now ready to take the derivative. The derivative acts trivially on all BP factors
z_{v,\lambda}
except at
A
. It also acts trivially on any loop that does not touch
A
. Thus, the derivative is nonvanishing only for clusters with support on
A
. For these clusters, we will use
\mathbf{W}_{\bar{A}}
to denote the subset of strings that do not intersect
A
; for
l\in\mathbf{W}_{\bar{A}}
, the normalized loop correction simplifies to

\frac{\left(\tilde{Z}_{{l},\lambda}^{A}\right)^{\eta_{l}}}{\prod_{v\in l}z_{v,\lambda}^{\eta_{l}}}=Z_{l}^{\eta_{l}}.

Thus, taking the derivative, we obtain,

\displaystyle\partial_{\lambda}\mathcal{Z}^{A}_{\lambda}\mid_{\lambda=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{z_{A}(O)}{z_{A}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{conn}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}\frac{\prod_{l\in\mathbf{W}_{\bar{A}}}Z_{l}^{\eta_{l}}}{\prod_{l\in\mathbf{W}_{A}}z_{v}^{\eta_{l}}}\left[\partial_{\lambda}\evaluated{\left(\prod_{l \in\mathbf{W}_A} \left(\nloopZ{l}\right)^{\eta_l}\right)}_{\lambda=0}-\prod_{l\in\mathbf{W}_{A}}Z_{l}^{\eta_{l}}\partial_{\lambda}\evaluated{\left(\prod_{l \in\mathbf{W}_A} z_{A,\lambda}^{\eta_l}\right)}_{\lambda=0}\right]

\displaystyle=\expectationvalue{O_A}_{BP}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{conn}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}\sum_{l\in\mathbf{W}_{A}}\eta_{l}\left[Z_{l}^{A}(Z_{l})^{\eta_{l}-1}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}l^{\prime}\in\mathbf{W}\\
l^{\prime}\neq l\end{subarray}}(Z_{l^{\prime}})^{\eta_{l^{\prime}}}-Z_{\mathbf{W}}\expectationvalue{O_A}_{BP}\right]

(S65a)

\displaystyle=\expectationvalue{O_A}_{BP}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{conn}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}Z_{\mathbf{W}}\sum_{l\in\mathbf{W}_{A}}\eta_{l}\left[\frac{Z_{l}^{A}}{Z_{l}}-\expectationvalue{O_A}_{BP}\right].

(S65b)

∎

Since
Z_{l}=0
for any open string, the term

Z_{l}^{A}(Z_{l})^{\eta_{l}-1}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}l^{\prime}\in\mathbf{W}\\
l^{\prime}\neq l\end{subarray}}(Z_{l^{\prime}})^{\eta_{l^{\prime}}}
in
Eq. (S65a)
can be non-vanishing only if all
l^{\prime}
are closed loops and either
l
is a closed loop, or it has multiplicity 1. The term
Z_{\mathbf{W}}\expectationvalue{O_A}_{BP}
can be non-vanishing only if
\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}
, i.e. consists of only closed loops.
Eq. (S65b)
is more concise, but care must be taken in the case where
Z_{l}^{A}
is nonvanishing but
Z_{l}
is zero.

The convergence of this expansion follows from
Eq. (S65a)
and the application of
Lemma S1.1
, as we now prove.

Theorem S3.2.

Given a local region
A\subset V
, assume decay of loops in
\mathcal{L}_{A}
on both
\mathcal{Z}=\langle\psi|\psi\rangle
and
\mathcal{Z}^{A}=\langle\psi|O_{A}|\psi\rangle
. Then, truncating the cluster expansion (derivative version) for local expectation values (
Proposition S3.2
) of an observable with with
\|O_{A}\|=1
in a region
A\subset V
leads to an additive error bounded by

|\expectationvalue{O_A}-\expectationvalue{O_A}_{m}|\leq\order{m |A|e^{-d(m+1)}},

(S66)

where
d=c-c_{0}
.

Proof.

By
Eq. (S65b)
, the additive error is

|\expectationvalue{O_A}_{m}-\expectationvalue{O_A}_{m}|=\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{conn}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
|\mathbf{W}|>m\end{subarray}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}Z_{\mathbf{W}}\sum_{l\in\mathbf{W}_{A}}\eta_{l}\left[\frac{Z_{l}^{A}}{Z_{l}}-\expectationvalue{O_A}_{BP}\right]\right|.

(S67)

We bound the sum by

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{conn}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
|\mathbf{W}|>m\end{subarray}}|\phi_{\mathbf{W}}Z_{\mathbf{W}}|\sum_{l\in\mathbf{W}_{A}}\eta_{l}\left|\frac{Z_{l}^{A}}{Z_{l}}-\expectationvalue{O_A}_{BP}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\order{\sum_{\substack{\text{conn} \, \mathbf{W} \leftarrow\LL_A \\
\supp(\mathbf{W}) \cap A \neq\emptyset\\ |\mathbf{W}| > m}} |\phi_{\mathbf{W}}| \max(|Z_{\mathbf{W}}|, |Z^A_{\mathbf{W}}|) \sum_{l \in\mathbf{W}_A} \eta_l}

\displaystyle\leq\order{\sum_{\substack{\text{conn} \, \mathbf{W} \leftarrow\LL_A \\
\supp(\mathbf{W}) \cap A \neq\emptyset\\ |\mathbf{W}| > m}} |\phi_{\mathbf{W}}| \max(|Z_{\mathbf{W}}|,|Z^A_{\mathbf{W}}|) |\mathbf{W}|}

(S68)

In going from the first to the second line, we upper bounded
|Z_{\mathbf{W}}||Z_{l}^{A}/Z_{l}-\expectationvalue{O_A}_{BP}|\leq|Z_{\mathbf{W}}\cdot Z_{l}^{A}/Z_{l}|+|Z_{\mathbf{W}}|\cdot|\expectationvalue{O_A}_{BP}|\leq\order{\max(|Z_\mathbf{W}|, |Z^A_{\mathbf{W}}|)}
since
|\expectationvalue{O_A}_{BP}|=|z_{v}(O_{A})/z_{v}|\leq\order{1}
(as
\|O_{A}\|=1
). In going from the second to the third line, we used the loose upper bound
\sum_{l\in\mathbf{W}_{A}}\eta_{l}\leq\sum_{l\in\mathbf{W}}\eta_{l}\leq\sum_{l\in\mathbf{W}}\eta_{l}|l|\leq|\mathbf{W}|
since
|l|>1
by definition.

Now we invoke
Lemma S1.1
, which can be restated as

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
|\mathbf{W}|=m\end{subarray}}|\phi_{\mathbf{W}}Z_{\mathbf{W}}|\leq\order{|A|e^{-(c-c_0)m}}.

(S69)

Therefore

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{conn}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
|\mathbf{W}|>m\end{subarray}}|\phi_{\mathbf{W}}Z_{\mathbf{W}}||\mathbf{W}|\leq\order{|A| \sum_{k=m+1}^\infty k e^{-(c-c_0) k}}\leq\order{m |A| e^{-d(m+1)}}.

(S70)

∎

S3.3
Region-based expansions

Now we turn to the regions-based version of the cluster cumulant expansion, used to obtain “order
k
” approximations to local observables in
subsection V.2
of the main text.

The region-finding step is modified from
Alg. 1
to
Alg. 2
. We assume that
A
is a single vertex; otherwise,
A
can be merged into a “supervertex.” The two main differences from
Alg. 1
are: (1) Only regions that contain
A
are included, and
A
can be a leaf of
R
(line 1). (2) When taking intersections,
P
is not required to be leafless, but all branches terminating on a vertex other than
A
can be pruned (line 9)
73
;
19
.

Input :
Graph
G
, integer
k
, vertex
A

Output :
Partially ordered set of regions
\mathcal{R}_{1},\mathcal{R}_{2},..
such that no region in
\mathcal{R}_{i}
is contained within a region in
\mathcal{R}_{j>i}

\mathcal{M}\leftarrow
connected, vertex-induced subgraphs of
G
that contain up to
k
vertices including
A
, and no other leaves;

1

\mathcal{R}_{1}\leftarrow

\{R\in\mathcal{M}\,|\,R\not\subset R^{\prime}\,\forall\,R^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}\}
(maximal/parent regions);

2

i\leftarrow
1;

3

while

\mathcal{R}_{i}
is not empty

do

4

\mathcal{R}_{i+1}\leftarrow\emptyset
;

5

for

R\in\mathcal{R}_{i}
,
R^{\prime}\in\mathcal{R}_{j\leq i}

do

6

P

\leftarrow
intersection of
R
and
R^{\prime}
;

7

if

P
is connected
and

P
contains
A

then

8

P\leftarrow
prune branches not ending on
A
;

9

if

P\notin\mathcal{R}_{i}

then

add
P
to
\mathcal{R}_{i+1}
;

10

end if

11

end for

12

i\leftarrow i+1
;

13

end while

14

15

return

\{\mathcal{R}_{1},...,\mathcal{R}_{i}\}
;

16

Algorithm 2

Region finding, local observables

Having obtained the pertinent regions, we turn to evaluating the derivative of the perturbed partition function. Here, we note that it is more convenient
not
to normalize the tensor network by the BP partition function. We will use
\widetilde{\Xi}_{\lambda}^{A}(R)
to denote the unnormalized partition function on region
R
, with the operator insertion
\exp(\lambda O_A)
. For maximal region size
k
, and an associated set of regions
\mathcal{R}
, we arrive at the approximation

\partial_{\lambda}\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda}^{A}\mid_{\lambda=0}\,\approx\sum_{R\in\mathcal{R}}b_{k}(R)\partial_{\lambda}\left(\log\widetilde{\Xi}_{\lambda}^{A}(R)\right)|_{\lambda=0}\,=\sum_{R\in\mathcal{R}}b_{k}(R)\expectationvalue{O_A}_{R}.

(S71)

This is the form of the expansion used for the finite-temperature numerics in the main text. It is the “weighted arithmetic mean” used in Ref.
49
, dubbed the “loop cluster sum formula” in Ref.
19
.

Ref.
19
also considers the “loop cluster product formula,” interpreted as a “weighted geometric mean” in Ref.
49
. That formula can be seen as the top-down, region-based version of our ratio expansion (
Eq. (S47)
):

\expectationvalue{O_A}=\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{A}}{\mathcal{Z}}\approx\frac{\exp\left[\sum_{R}b_{k}(R)\log\widetilde{\Xi}_{A}(R)\right]}{\exp\left[\sum_{R}b_{k}(R)\log\widetilde{\Xi}(R)\right]}=\prod_{R}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\Xi}_{A}(R)}{\widetilde{\Xi}(R)}\right)^{b_{k}(R)}=\prod_{R}(\langle O_{A}\rangle_{R})^{b_{k}(R)}.

(S72)

S3.4
A note on loop vs. string decay

We conclude this section by commenting upon the relationship between loop decay in the norm network
\mathcal{Z}
and the decay of excitations on
\mathcal{Z}^{A}
.

Suppose the tensor network
\mathcal{Z}
satisfies
c
-decay with
c>c_{0}
. Does the cluster expansion for an arbitrary local observable
O_{A}
converge? To answer this question, consider a string
l
in the excitation set
\mathcal{L}_{A}
. To guarantee a convergent expansion, a sufficient condition is (1)
|Z_{l}|\leq\order{e^{-c|l|}}
and (2)
|Z_{l}^{A}|\leq\order{e^{-c_A |l|}}
with
c_{A}>c_{0}
.

If
|Z_{l}|
is nonzero, the first inequality implies the second, with
c_{A}=c
: changing a local region does not modify the rate of exponential decay. However, if
|Z_{l}|=0
, then (1) is trivially satisfied, but
|Z_{{l},\lambda}^{A}|
need not decay for
\lambda\neq 0
. Open strings in
\mathcal{L}_{A}/\mathcal{L}
present one example of this caveat: the fact that
|Z_{l}|=0
for such strings follows directly from the fixed point condition, but says nothing about the decay when the tensor network is perturbed.

Despite these caveats, in states with clustering of correlations, we
do
expect loop decay on
\mathcal{Z}
and string decay on
\mathcal{Z}^{A}
to be parametrically similar. Indeed, we can think of
c-c_{0}
as an effective “string tension” which is stable under local perturbations. For the same reason, the performance of the expansion will generally be insensitive to the choice of bounded observable. The exception is again when certain sets of loops/strings are identically zero: for example, in the PEPS of the 2D classical Ising model,
\expectationvalue{X}_{BP}=0
and all loop corrections vanish for any choice of fixed point, including “poor” fixed points. It would be interesting if one could observe different decay patterns of strings on
\mathcal{L}_{A}
for different choices of local observables. This may lead to certain observables being “harder” to estimate than others, e.g., order parameters near a phase transition.

S4
Correlation Functions

We can now apply a similar reasoning to connected correlation functions
\expectationvalue{O_AO_B}_{c}:=\expectationvalue{O_AO_B}-\expectationvalue{O_A}\expectationvalue{O_B}
, followed by a generalization to
p
-pointed connected correlations.

S4.1
Ratio of partition functions

Proposition S4.1.

Let
\psi
be a state represented as a PEPS on
G=(V,E)
. The ratio form of the cluster expansion for connected correlators is given as,

\displaystyle\expectationvalue{O_AO_B}_{c}

\displaystyle=\expectationvalue{O_A}\expectationvalue{O_B}\left[\exp\!\Biggl\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\emph{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{AB}\\
\emph{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
\emph{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap B\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}\left(Z^{AB}_{\mathbf{W}}+Z_{\mathbf{W}}-Z^{A}_{\mathbf{W}}-Z^{B}_{\mathbf{W}}\right)\Biggr\}-1\right]

(S73)

where
Z_{\mathbf{W}}^{X}
refers to evaluation of the cluster
\mathbf{W}
on the network
\mathcal{Z}_{X}=\langle\psi|O_{X}|\psi\rangle
, where
O_{X}=O_{A(B)}
for
X=A(B)
and
O_{X}=O_{A}O_{B}
for
X=AB
.

Proof.

Now, note that
\expectationvalue{O_AO_B}_{c}=\expectationvalue{O_AO_B}-\expectationvalue{O_A}\expectationvalue{O_B}
. The individual parts are, by repeated application of
Proposition S3.1
,

\displaystyle\expectationvalue{O_A}

\displaystyle=\expectationvalue{O_A}_{\text{BP}}\cdot\exp{\left(\sum_{\substack{\text{connected} \, \mathbf{W} \leftarrow\LL_A \\
\supp(\mathbf{W}) \cap A \neq\emptyset}} \phi_{\mathbf{W}} (Z^A_{\mathbf{W}} - Z_{\mathbf{W}})\right)}

(S74)

\displaystyle\expectationvalue{O_B}

\displaystyle=\expectationvalue{O_B}_{\text{BP}}\cdot\exp{\left(\sum_{\substack{\text{connected} \, \mathbf{W} \leftarrow\LL_B \\
\supp(\mathbf{W}) \cap B \neq\emptyset}} \phi_{\mathbf{W}} (Z^B_{\mathbf{W}} - Z_{\mathbf{W}})\right)}

(S75)

\displaystyle\expectationvalue{O_AO_B}

\displaystyle=\expectationvalue{O_A}_{\text{BP}}\expectationvalue{O_B}_{\text{BP}}\cdot\exp{\left(\sum_{\substack{\text{connected} \, \mathbf{W} \leftarrow\LL_{AB} \\
\supp(\mathbf{W}) \cap\{A,B\} \neq\emptyset}} \phi_{\mathbf{W}} (Z^{AB}_{\mathbf{W}} - Z_{\mathbf{W}})\right)}

(S76)

We note the following,

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{AB}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap\{A,B\}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{AB}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap\{A\}=\emptyset\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap\{B\}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{AB}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap\{A\}\neq\emptyset\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap\{B\}=\emptyset\end{subarray}}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{AB}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap\{A\}\neq\emptyset\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap\{B\}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}

(S77)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap\{A\}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap\{A\}\neq\emptyset\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap\{B\}=\emptyset\end{subarray}}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap\{A\}\neq\emptyset\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap\{B\}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}

(S78)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{B}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap\{B\}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{B}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap\{A\}=\emptyset\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap\{B\}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{B}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap\{A\}\neq\emptyset\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap\{B\}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}

(S79)

Since
\mathcal{T}^{AB}
differs from
\mathcal{T}^{A(B)}
only at
B(A)
, we get,

\displaystyle\expectationvalue{O_AO_B}_{c}

\displaystyle=\expectationvalue{O_A}_{\text{BP}}\expectationvalue{O_B}_{\text{BP}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f_{A}}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f_{B}}\times

\displaystyle\biggl[\exp\!\Biggl\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{AB}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap B\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}(Z^{AB}_{\mathbf{W}}-Z_{\mathbf{W}})\Biggr\}-

(S80)

\displaystyle\exp\!\Biggl\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap B\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}(Z^{A}_{\mathbf{W}}-Z_{\mathbf{W}})\Biggr\}\exp\!\Biggl\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{B}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap B\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}(Z^{B}_{\mathbf{W}}-Z_{\mathbf{W}})\Biggr\}\biggr]

(S81)

with (this factors out since, e.g, for
\mathbf{W}
supported exclusively on
A
but not
B
, we have
Z_{\mathbf{W}}^{AB}=Z_{\mathbf{W}}^{A}
)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f_{A}}

\displaystyle=\exp{\textcolor{blue}{\sum_{\substack{\text{connected} \, \mathbf{W} \leftarrow\LL_{AB} \\ \supp(\mathbf{W}) \cap\{A\} \neq\emptyset\\ \supp(\mathbf{W}) \cap\{B\} = \emptyset}}} \phi_{\mathbf{W}}(Z^{A}_\mathbf{W} - Z_\mathbf{W})}

(S82)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f_{B}}

\displaystyle=\exp{ \textcolor{red}{\sum_{\substack{\text{connected} \, \mathbf{W} \leftarrow\LL_{AB} \\ \supp(\mathbf{W}) \cap\{A\} = \emptyset\\ \supp(\mathbf{W}) \cap\{B\} \neq\emptyset}}} \phi_{\mathbf{W}}(Z^{B}_\mathbf{W} - Z_\mathbf{W})}

(S83)

Factoring out further the local terms, we thus arrive at,

\displaystyle\expectationvalue{O_AO_B}_{c}

\displaystyle=\expectationvalue{O_A}\expectationvalue{O_B}\left[\exp\!\Biggl\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{AB}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap B\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}\left(Z^{AB}_{\mathbf{W}}+Z_{\mathbf{W}}-Z^{A}_{\mathbf{W}}-Z^{B}_{\mathbf{W}}\right)\Biggr\}-1\right]

(S84)

where we used,

\displaystyle\expectationvalue{O_A}

\displaystyle=\expectationvalue{O_A}_{\text{BP}}\cdot\exp{\left(\sum_{\substack{\text{connected} \, \mathbf{W} \leftarrow\LL_A \\
\supp(\mathbf{W}) \cap A \neq\emptyset}} \phi_{\mathbf{W}} (Z^A_{\mathbf{W}} - Z_{\mathbf{W}})\right)}

(S85)

\displaystyle\equiv\expectationvalue{O_A}_{\text{BP}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f_{A}}\cdot\exp\!\Biggl\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap B\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}(Z^{A}_{\mathbf{W}}-Z_{\mathbf{W}})\Biggr\}

(S86)

and similarly for
O_{B}
.
∎

This can again be re-organized into a cluster-cumulant expansion as follows.

Corollary S4.1.

Let
\psi
be a state represented as a PEPS on
\Lambda
. The cumulant-cluster expansion for connected correlators is given as,

\displaystyle\expectationvalue{O_AO_B}_{c}

\displaystyle=\expectationvalue{O_A}\expectationvalue{O_B}\left[\exp\!\Biggl\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\emph{conn.}\,\Gamma\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{AB}\\
\emph{supp}(\Gamma)\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
\emph{supp}(\Gamma)\cap B\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\left(\mathfrak{K}^{AB}_{\mathbf{W}}+\mathfrak{K}_{\mathbf{W}}-\mathfrak{K}^{A}_{\mathbf{W}}-\mathfrak{K}^{B}_{\mathbf{W}}\right)\Biggr\}-1\right]

(S87)

The cluster expansion for connected correlators can then be used to show that decay of loops ensures clustering of bipartite correlations as follows.

Theorem S4.1.

Decay of loops in
\mathcal{L}_{AB}
on the networks
\mathcal{Z}^{X}
for
X\in\{\emptyset,A,B,AB\}
ensures that bipartite connected correlators
\expectationvalue{O_AO_B}_{c}:=\expectationvalue{O_AO_B}-\expectationvalue{O_A}\expectationvalue{O_B}
for bounded local observables
O_{A(B)}
with
\emph{supp}(O_{A(B)})\subseteq A(B)
and
\|O_{A(B)}\|=1
cluster,

\Bigl|\expectationvalue{O_AO_B}_{c}\Bigr|\leq\order{e^{-d(A,B) / \xi} }

for a finite correlation length
\xi\leq\order{(1/(c-c_0))}
and
d(A,B)
being the graph distance.

Proof.

We recall, by
Proposition S4.1

\displaystyle\expectationvalue{O_AO_B}_{c}

\displaystyle=\expectationvalue{O_A}\expectationvalue{O_B}\left[\exp\!\Biggl\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{AB}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap B\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}\left(Z^{AB}_{\mathbf{W}}+Z_{\mathbf{W}}-Z^{A}_{\mathbf{W}}-Z^{B}_{\mathbf{W}}\right)\Biggr\}-1\right]

(S88)

Now,

\displaystyle\Bigl|\expectationvalue{O_AO_B}_{c}\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\expectationvalue{O_A}\expectationvalue{O_B}\left[\exp\!\Biggl\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{AB}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap B\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\left|\phi_{\mathbf{W}}\left(Z^{AB}_{\mathbf{W}}+Z_{\mathbf{W}}-Z^{A}_{\mathbf{W}}-Z^{B}_{\mathbf{W}}\right)\right|\Biggr\}-1\right]\Big|

(S89)

\displaystyle\leq|\expectationvalue{O_A}||\expectationvalue{O_B}|\left[\exp\!\Biggl\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{AB}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap B\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\Bigl|\phi_{\mathbf{W}}\Bigr|\,\Bigl(\,|Z^{AB}_{\mathbf{W}}|+|Z_{\mathbf{W}}|+|Z^{A}_{\mathbf{W}}|+|Z^{B}_{\mathbf{W}}|\,\Bigr)\Biggr\}-1\right]

(S90)

\displaystyle=\|O_{A}\|\|O_{B}\|\left[\exp\!\Biggl\{\sum_{X\in\{\emptyset,A,B,AB\}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{AB}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap B\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\Bigl|\phi_{\mathbf{W}}Z^{X}_{\mathbf{W}}\Bigr|\Biggr\}-1\right]

(S91)

\displaystyle=\left[\prod_{X\in\{\emptyset,A,B,AB\}}\exp\!\Biggl\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{connected}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{AB}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap B\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\Bigl|\phi_{\mathbf{W}}Z^{X}_{\mathbf{W}}\Bigr|\Biggr\}-1\right].

(S92)

where we used
|e^{x}-1|\leq e^{|x|}-1
, and the fact that
\|O_{A(B)}\|=1
. We have that for all
X\in\{\emptyset,A,B,AB\}
and for all excitations
l\in\mathcal{L}_{AB}
,

|Z^{X}_{l}|\leq\order{ e^{-c|l|}}

(S94)

with
c>c_{0}
. Since all clusters with support on both
A
and
B
have weight at least
d(A,B)
, we get,

\displaystyle\Bigl|\expectationvalue{O_AO_B}_{c}\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\left[\prod_{X\in\{\emptyset,A,B,AB\}}\exp\!\Biggl\{r|AB|e^{-(c-c_{0})(d(A,B)+1)}\Biggr\}-1\right]

(S95)

\displaystyle\leq\left[\exp\!\Biggl\{4r|AB|e^{-(c-c_{0})(d(A,B)+1)}\Biggr\}-1\right]

(S96)

\displaystyle\leq 4r|AB|e^{-(c-c_{0})(d(A,B)+1)}\exp\!\bigl\{4r|AB|e^{-(c-c_{0})(d(A,B)+1)}\bigr\}.

(S97)

where
r=\order{1}
is a constant and we used,

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{conn}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{A}\\
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\ni A\\
|\mathbf{W}|>m\end{subarray}}|\phi_{\mathbf{W}}Z^{X}_{\mathbf{W}}|\leq\order{|A|e^{-(c-c_0)(m+1)}}

(S98)

by
Lemma S1.1
and the fact that
e^{x}-1\leq xe^{x}
.

Since
e^{-(c-c_{0})(d(A,B)+1)}\to 0
exponentially as
d(A,B)\to\infty
, for all
d(A,B)
large enough we have
4re^{-(c-c_{0})(d(A,B)+1)}\leq 1
, and hence there is a
C=\order{|AB|}
such that

\Bigl|\expectationvalue{O_AO_B}_{c}\Bigr|\leq Ce^{-(c-c_{0})d(A,B)}.

Thus, we have clustering of correlations,

\Bigl|\expectationvalue{O_AO_B}_{c}\Bigr|\;\leq\;\order{e^{-d(A,B)/\xi}},\qquad\text{with }\xi\leq\order{\frac{1}{c-c_0}}.

∎

S4.2
Derivative of free energy

Here, we provide the additive version of cluster expansion for
p-
point connected correlation functions.

Proposition S4.2.

Let
\psi
be a state represented as a PEPS on
G=(V,E)
. Given local disjoint regions
A_{i}\subset V
, and observables
O_{A_{i}}
satisfying
\emph{supp}(O_{A_{i}})\subseteq A_{i}
, we have the following cluster expansion for the
p-
point connected correlation functions,

\expectationvalue{O_{A_1}\dots O_{A_p}}_{c}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\emph{conn.}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{A}}\\
\emph{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A_{i}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}\partial_{\bm{\lambda}}Z^{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{W},\bm{\lambda}}\Big|_{\bm{\lambda}=0}

(S99)

where we denote
\bm{\lambda}:=(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{p})
,
\partial_{\bm{\lambda}}:=\prod_{i}\partial_{\lambda_{i}}
,
\mathbf{A}=(A_{1},\dots,A_{p})
and the cluster evaluation
Z^{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{W},\bm{\lambda}}
is done over the network
\mathcal{Z}_{\bm{\lambda}}^{\mathbf{A}}:=\langle\psi|\exp(\sum_i O_{A_i}\lambda_i)|\psi\rangle
. The clusters
\mathbf{W}
are constructed over
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{A}}
, the set of connected sub-graphs required to be degree
\geq 2
everywhere except in all regions
A_{i}
.

Proof.

A standard result in statistical mechanics (see, e.g., Ref.
16
) treats the free energy as a cumulant generating function, whose derivatives are the connected correlation functions:

\expectationvalue{O_{A_1}\dots O_{A_p}}_{c}=-\partial_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathcal{F}_{\bm{\lambda}}^{\mathbf{A}}\Big|_{\bm{\lambda}=0}

(S100)

where
\mathcal{F}_{\bm{\lambda}}^{\mathbf{A}}:=-\log\langle\psi|\exp(\sum_i O_{A_i}\lambda_i)|\psi\rangle
. When
p=1
, this reduces to the expectation value
\expectationvalue{O_A}
, while for
p=2
, we recover
\expectationvalue{O_{A_1}O_{A_2}}_{c}=\expectationvalue{O_{A_1} O_{A_2}}-\expectationvalue{O_{A_1}}\expectationvalue{O_{A_2}}
. Now, we apply the cluster expansion to
\mathcal{F}_{\bm{\lambda}}^{\mathbf{A}}
. Owing to the
\bm{\lambda}
derivatives, the only clusters with a non-zero contribution are those which intersect all of the regions
A_{i}
, leading to the condition
\text{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A_{i}\neq\emptyset
for all
i\in[p]
.
∎

Following a similar approach to
Sec. S3.2
, we can explicitly evaluate
Eq. (S99)
for the most physically relevant case of
p=2
. A (tedious) calculation yields

\displaystyle\expectationvalue{O_A O_B}_{c}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\lambda_{a}}\partial_{\lambda_{b}}\mathcal{Z}_{(\lambda_{a},\lambda_{b})}^{AB}\mid_{\lambda_{a}=\lambda_{b}=0}\,=\partial_{\lambda_{a}}\partial_{\lambda_{b}}\evaluated{\left(\sum_{\substack{\mathrm{conn.} \, \mathbf{W} \leftarrow\LL_{AB} \\ \mathrm{supp}(\mathbf{W}) \cap A \neq\emptyset\\ \mathrm{supp} (\mathbf{W}) \cap B \neq\emptyset}} \phi_{\mathbf{W}} \tloopZ{\mathbf{W}}\right)}_{\lambda_a=\lambda_b=0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathrm{conn.}\,\mathbf{W}\leftarrow\mathcal{L}_{AB}\\
\mathrm{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap A\neq\emptyset\\
\mathrm{supp}(\mathbf{W})\cap B\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\phi_{\mathbf{W}}Z_{\mathbf{W}}\left[\sum_{l\in\mathbf{W}_{AB}}\eta_{l}\left(\frac{Z_{l}^{AB}}{Z_{l}}+(\eta_{l}-1)\frac{Z_{l}^{A}Z_{l}^{B}}{Z_{l}^{2}}\right)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l\in\mathbf{W}_{A}\\
l^{\prime}\in\mathbf{W}_{B}\\
l^{\prime}\neq l\end{subarray}}\eta_{l}\eta_{l^{\prime}}\frac{Z_{l}^{A}Z_{l^{\prime}}^{B}}{Z_{l}Z_{l^{\prime}}}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l\in\mathbf{W}_{A}\\
l^{\prime}\in\mathbf{W}_{B}\end{subarray}}\eta_{l}\eta_{l^{\prime}}\expectationvalue{O_A}_{BP}\expectationvalue{O_B}_{BP}\right].

(S101)

Here,
\mathbf{W}_{A}
and
\mathbf{W}_{B}
denote the subsets of loops in
\mathbf{W}
that intersect
A
and
B
, respectively, while
\mathbf{W}_{AB}
denotes the subset of strings that intersect both
A
and
B
. We emphasize that
\mathbf{W}_{AB}
may be non-empty, as in the case of a cluster
\mathbf{W}=\{(l_{A},1),(l_{B},1)\}
where
l_{A}(B)
intersects
A(B)
and the two strings intersect in the bulk.
Z_{l}^{AB},Z_{l}^{A},
and
Z_{l}^{B}
denote the normalized weights of loop
l
on the tensor networks
\mathcal{Z}^{AB},\mathcal{Z}^{A},
and
\mathcal{Z}^{B}
, respectively.

S5
Additional numerics

Here we present additional numerical results on ground states and at finite temperature.

S5.1
Ground state iPEPS

The iPEPS ground state is obtained through CTMRG-based variational optimization on a
C_{4}-
symmetric representation with bond dimension
D=4
using
peps-torch
. The optimization is approximate, and the apparent critical point is obtained at
h_{x}=3.06
, vs. the high-precision estimate of
h_{x}^{c}=3.044330(6)
obtained via quantum Monte Carlo
26
. The message-passing fixed points are obtained through iterating the equation
\mu\to T\star\mu^{\otimes 3}/\|T\star\mu^{\otimes 3}\|_{2}
until convergence, starting from the all-ones vector. To locate the unstable fixed point for
h_{x}\in(3.06,3.2)
[in the regime where the CTMRG magnetization vanishes but the BP magnetization does not], we employ a damped Newton search initialized using the fixed point at
h_{x}=3.2
. The initialization consists of 500 random seeds drawn from a Gaussian cloud with standard deviation
0.5
centered around this reference fixed point. We verify that the newly discovered (approximate) fixed point is unstable by perturbing it slightly and observing convergence towards the stable (symmetry-broken) fixed point under message-passing.

S5.2
Decay of loops

In the main text, we claimed that odd-
|l|
loops are strongly suppressed compared to even
|l|
loops. This suppression comes from degree 3 vertices, which are necessarily present in odd-
|l|
generalized loops.
Fig. S1
a-b shows the scaling of maximum-weight odd and even loops in the 3D and 2D TFIM, respectively, at fixed high temperature. For a given
|l|
, the loops of highest weight are those with the minimal number of degree-3 vertices, which is 0(1) for even(odd) loops. Thus, both parities scale approximately as
\tanh(\beta)^{|l|}
, with a constant prefactor that differs based on parity. As
h_{x}
increases, the gap between odd and even loops narrows.

Figure S1:
Decay of loops around a BP fixed point in the transverse field Ising model. (a) 3D TFIM at inverse temperature
\beta=0.2
, transverse field
h_{x}=1,2,3,4
. (b) 2D TFIM at inverse temperature
\beta=0.25
, with
h_{x}
ranging from 0.25 to 3 in increments of 0.25. In (a) and (b), the gray lines have slope
-\log(\tanh(\beta))
, which is the classical scaling of loops with respect to the paramagnetic fixed point. (c) 2D TFIM at
\beta=0.65
, which is in the ordered phase for the transverse fields shown (up to
h_{x}=2.25
). Solid and dashed lines indicate loops of maximal and minimal weight, respectively. Dashed gray line indicates the classical scaling with slope
\log(e^{4\beta}-1)
.

Fig. S1
c shows the scaling of loops around the ferromagnetic fixed point, at low temperatures in the 2D TFIM. Here, two distinct scalings emerge. Maximal weight loops (solid lines) have the maximal number of degree-4 and degree-3 vertices, owing to the broken symmetry of the fixed point which favors the clustering of excitations. In the classical 2D Ising model, each degree-4 vertex carries a weight of
1-\frac{1}{2\sinh^{2}(2\beta)}
. Meanwhile, minimal-weight loops (dashed lines) have the maximal number of degree-2 vertices, and scale as
(e^{4\beta}-1)^{-|l|}
.

S5.3
Cluster-cumulant expansion

Figure S2:
Same as
Fig. 3
b-c in the main text, but for
|\langle S_{z}\rangle|
instead of
\expectationvalue{S_x}
. (a) shows
|\langle S_{z}\rangle|
as a function of
\beta
at
h_{x}=0.25
, evaluated using CTMRG (black squares), vs. increasing orders of the region-based cumulant cluster expansion (ranging from
k=1
in light orange, to
k=11
in dark red). (b) shows the additive error in the cumulant cluster expansion, using CTMRG as the “ground truth.” Dashed gray line marks the inverse temperature at which
\expectationvalue{S_x}_{\rm CTMRG}
has a peak, while the dotted gray line is the inverse critical temperature
\beta_{c}\approx 0.446
.

In the main text, we presented data on the region-based cluster cumulant expansion for the observable
S_{x}
as a function of inverse temperature
\beta
. As a complement to
Fig. 3
c-d,
Fig. S2
shows the corresponding data for the observable
S_{z}
. All states were prepared with a small longitudinal field
h_{z}=10^{-5}
, so that
\langle S_{z}\rangle
is slightly positive in the paramagnetic phase. We chose this field to bias the ferromagnetic phase towards a definite sign in the magnetization, while keeping the true phase transition fairly sharp. At this value of the longitudinal field, both BP and CTMRG still sometimes converge toward a negative magnetization instead, and thus we plot
\left|\langle S_{z}\rangle\right|
. The error has a broad peak within the “confusion regime”, reaching a maximum near the true critical temperature (dotteed line). We observe that the peak in
\expectationvalue{S_x}
and its corresponding error occurs at a slightly higher temperature (
\beta=0.41
, dashed line) than the actual phase transition (estimated as
\beta_{c}(0.25)\approx 0.446
in Ref.
32
).

This general behavior – peak in
\expectationvalue{S_x}
coinciding with a peak in the
\left|\expectationvalue{S_x}_{k}-\expectationvalue{S_x}_{\mathrm{CTMRG}}\right|
followed soon after by symmetry breaking in
\expectationvalue{S_z}
coinciding with a peak in
\Big||\langle{S_{z}}\rangle_{k}|-|\langle{S_{z}}\rangle_{\mathrm{CTMRG}}|\Big|
– persists to larger transverse field. All four quantities are plotted for
g=1.5
and
g=2.75
in
Fig. S3
and
Fig. S4
, respectively.

Figure S3:
Comparison of regions-based cluster cumulant expansion up to
k=10
vs. CTMRG, on Gibbs states of the 2D TFIM at
h_{x}=1.5
. (a), (b): expectation values and error in
\expectationvalue{S_x}
. (c), (d): expectation values and error in
|\langle S_{z}\rangle|
. In all panels, the dashed line indicates the inverse temperature at which
\expectationvalue{S_x}_{\mathrm{CTMRG}}
has a peak, while the dotted line is the inverse critical temperature from Ref.
32
.

Figure S4:
Same as
Fig. S3
, but for
h_{x}=2.75
.
